1. **Identify all paths between F and C:**
   - Path 1: F -> B -> S -> N -> C
   - Path 2: F -> B -> G -> C
   - Path 3: F -> B -> T -> G -> C
   - Path 4: F -> B -> T -> I -> J -> G -> C
   - Path 5: F -> B -> T -> R -> O -> Q -> J -> G -> C

2. **Find the minimum cut set:**
   - Removing edge (F, B) will disconnect Paths 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5.
   - Alternatively, other strategic removals can be considered, but removing (F, B) is the most efficient.

However, since removing (F, B) alone does not suffice due to the presence of alternative paths, we need to consider removing additional edges.

3. **Consider additional edges to remove:**
   - Removing (F, B) and (B, T) will disconnect most paths, but we need to ensure all paths are cut.
   - Removing (F, B), (B, T), and (T, I) will ensure that all paths are disrupted.

Thus, the minimum number of edges that must be removed is 3.

### Final Answer:
Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(F, B), (B, T), (T, I)].

1. \( S \rightarrow E \rightarrow B \rightarrow T \rightarrow H \rightarrow G \rightarrow C \rightarrow N \rightarrow R \)
2. \( S \rightarrow E \rightarrow B \rightarrow T \rightarrow H \rightarrow G \rightarrow J \rightarrow R \)
3. \( S \rightarrow E \rightarrow B \rightarrow T \rightarrow F \rightarrow A \rightarrow N \rightarrow R \)
4. \( S \rightarrow E \rightarrow B \rightarrow G \rightarrow C \rightarrow N \rightarrow R \)
5. \( S \rightarrow E \rightarrow B \rightarrow G \rightarrow J \rightarrow R \)
6. \( S \rightarrow E \rightarrow F \rightarrow A \rightarrow N \rightarrow R \)

Now, let's find the minimal cut set:

- Removing \( E \) will break paths 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, and 6.
- Removing \( B \) will break paths 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5.
- Removing \( T \) will break paths 1, 2, and 3.
- Removing \( G \) will break paths 1, 2, 4, and 5.
- Removing \( N \) will break paths 1, 3, and 6.
- Removing \( C \) will break paths 1 and 4.
- Removing \( J \) will break paths 2 and 5.
- Removing \( A \) will break paths 3 and 6.

To minimize the number of nodes to remove, we need to find the smallest set of nodes that covers all paths. By analyzing the paths, we see that removing \( E \) alone is sufficient to break all paths between \( S \) and \( R \).
